OUTPUT: set turn="review" and return exactly one item in the "review" array; leave every other array empty.
REVIEW TURN. Independently audit EVERY candidate in `candidates`; each is a
hypothesis from the discovery turn, not a trusted conclusion. Return exactly one
decision per candidate_id.

Return obligation_class (execution_compatibility per D4, target_coverage per D5),
scope_basis, trigger_basis and the five checks of J1, each with status
established/refuted/unknown, a specific reason and exact evidence:
scope_contract (J1a, D2); feasible_trigger (J1b): for execution compatibility a
finite actual target execution, for target coverage a valid supported
behavior/setup whose route the target blocks; observable_consequence (J1c);
alternatives_ruled_out (J1d, D8), including counterevidence supplied with the
candidate; identity_preserved (J1e; for timing also T1).

Use effect blocked_target_coverage only for target_coverage. For target_coverage
fill every coverage_witness field; for the other class use empty strings. These
fields do not replace the citations of each check.

decision=keep requires all five checks established with source and harness
evidence, a finite_witness and no decisive missing fact. decision=dismiss requires
a grounded refutation or equivalent route (J6). Otherwise return uncertain and
request the missing facts (E3). For maintenance, unresolved timing does not
invalidate a currently established obligation (J2).

Then audit the whole scope again (J4): assess every selected change ID and the
four foundations with evidence, challenge both dismissals (J6) and retained
gaps (J1). Out-of-scope items (D7) are not obligations. New evidence contains
contracts retrieved for earlier requests (`evidence`); `citation_errors` lists earlier
citations the program could not verify; cite valid locations instead. Request
exact symbols or paths only for decisive missing facts (E3).
If the evidence establishes a distinct obligation that is NOT among `candidates`
(for example one that only became visible with the new evidence), report it in
`new_candidates` with the same fields as a discovery blocker and mark its change
group as blocked in the scope audit. It is reviewed in a separate turn; do not
add a decision for it here.

0023\n674|   * 0013 FORK 0019\n675|   * 0015 LOADK 1\n676|   * 0017 JUMP 0021\n677|   * 0019 LOADK 2\n678|   * 0021 JUMP 0025\n679|   * 0023 LOADK 3\n680|   * 0025 JUMP 0029\n681|   * 0027 LOADK 4\n682|   *\n683|   * That's: N-1 commas for N elements, N LOADKs, and a JUMP between\n684|   * every LOADK.  The sequence ends in a LOADK.  Any deviation and it's\n685|   * not a list of constants.\n686|   *\n687|   * Here we check for this pattern almost exactly.  We don't check that\n688|   * the targets of the FORK and JUMP instructions are in the right\n689|   * sequence.\n690|   */\n691|  int all_const = 1;\n692|  int commas = 0;\n693|  int normal = 1;\n694|  jv a = jv_array();\n695|  for (inst *i = expr.first; i; i = i->next) {\n696|    if (i->op == FORK) {\n697|      commas++;\n698|      if (i->imm.target == NULL || i->imm.target->op != JUMP ||\n699|          jv_array_length(jv_copy(a)) > 0) {\n700|        normal = 0;\n701|        break;\n702|      }\n703|    } else if (all_const && i->op == LOADK) {\n704|      if (i->next != NULL && i->next->op != JUMP) {\n705|        normal = 0;\n706|        break;\n707|      }\n708|      a = jv_array_append(a, jv_copy(i->imm.constant));\n709|    } else if (i->op != JUMP || i->imm.target == NULL ||\n710|               i->imm.target->op != LOADK) {\n711|      all_const = 0;\n712|    }\n713|  }\n714|\n715|  if (all_const && normal &&\n716|      (expr.last == NULL || expr.last->op == LOADK) &&\n717|      jv_array_length(jv_copy(a)) == commas + 1) {\n718|    block_free(expr);\n719|    return gen_const(a);\n720|  }\n721|\n722|  jv_free(a);\n723|  block b = {0,0};\n724|  return b;\n725|}\n726|\n727|block gen_collect(block expr) {\n728|  block const_array = gen_const_array(expr);\n729|  if (const_array.first != NULL)\n730|    return const_array;\n731|\n732|  block array_var = gen_op_var_fresh(STOREV, \"collect\");\n733|  block c = BLOCK(gen_op_simple(DUP), gen_const(jv_array()), array_var);\n734|\n735|  block tail = BLOCK(gen_op_bound(APPEND, array_var),\n736|                     gen_op_simple(BACKTRACK));\n737|\n738|  return BLOCK(c,\n739|               gen_op_target(FORK, tail),\n740|               expr,\n741|               tail,\n742|               gen_op_bound(LOADVN, array_var));\n743|}\n744|\n745|static block bind_matcher(block matcher, block body) {\n746|  // cannot call block_bind(matcher, body) because that requires\n747|  // block_has_only_binders(matcher), which is not true here as matchers\n748|  // may also contain code to extract the correct elements\n749|  for (inst* i = matcher.first; If that code raises an exception, then TRY_END will wrap\n1049|   * and re-raise that exception, and TRY_BEGIN will unwrap and re-raise the\n1050|   * exception (see jq_next()).\n1051|   *\n1052|   * If <exp> raises then the TRY_BEGIN will see a non-wrapped exception and\n1053|   * will jump to the handler (note the TRY_END will not execute in this case),\n1054|   * and if the handler produces any values, then we'll execute whatever\n1055|   * bytecode follows this sequence.  Note that TRY_END will not execute in\n1056|   * this case, so if the handler raises an exception, or code past the handler\n1057|   * raises an exception, then that exception won't be wrapped and re-raised,\n1058|   * and the TRY_BEGIN will not catch it because it does not stack_save() when\n1059|   * it branches to the handler.\n1060|   */\n1061|\n1062|  if (block_is_noop(handler))\n1063|    handler = BLOCK(gen_op_simple(DUP), gen_op_simple(POP));\n1064|\n1065|  block jump = gen_op_target(JUMP, handler);\n1066|  return BLOCK(gen_op_target(TRY_BEGIN, jump), exp, gen_op_simple(TRY_END),\n1067|               jump, handler);\n1068|}\n1069|\n1070|block gen_label(const char *label, block exp) {\n1071|  block cond = gen_call(\"_equal\",\n1072|                        BLOCK(gen_lambda(gen_noop()),\n1073|                              gen_lambda(gen_op_unbound(LOADV, label))));\n1074|  return gen_wildvar_binding(gen_op_simple(GENLABEL), label,\n1075|                             BLOCK(gen_op_simple(POP),\n1076|                                   // try exp catch if . == $label\n1077|                                   //               then empty\n1078|                                   //               else error end\n1079|                                   //\n1080|                                   // Can't use gen_binop(), as that's firmly\n1081|                                   // stuck in parser.y as it refers to things\n1082|                                   // like EQ.\n1083|                                   gen_try(exp,\n1084|                                           gen_cond(cond,\n1085|                                                    gen_op_simple(BACKTRACK),\n1086|                                                    gen_call(\"error\", gen_noop())))));\n1087|}\n1088|\n1089|block gen_cbinding(const struct cfunction* cfunctions, int ncfunctions, block code) {\n1090|  for (int cfunc=0; We want timegm(), which isn't standard.\n1442| *\n1443| * To make things worse, mktime() tells you what the timezone\n1444| * adjustment is, but you have to #define _BSD_SOURCE to get this\n1445| * field of struct tm on some systems.\n1446| *\n1447| * This is all to blame on POSIX, of course.\n1448| *\n1449| * Our wrapper tries to use timegm() if available, or mktime() and\n1450| * correct for its side-effects if possible.\n1451| *\n1452| * Returns (time_t)-2 if mktime()'s side-effects cannot be corrected.\n1453| */\n1454|static time_t my_mktime(struct tm *tm) {\n1455|#ifdef HAVE_TIMEGM\n1456|  return timegm(tm);\n1457|#elif HAVE_TM_TM_GMT_OFF\n1458|\n1459|  time_t t = mktime(tm);\n1460|  if (t == (time_t)-1)\n1461|    return t;\n1462|  return t + tm->tm_gmtoff;\n1463|#elif HAVE_TM___TM_GMT_OFF\n1464|  time_t t = mktime(tm);\n1465|  if (t == (time_t)-1)\n1466|    return t;\n1467|  return t + tm->__tm_gmtoff;\n1468|#elif WIN32\n1469|  return _mkgmtime(tm);\n1470|#else\n1471|  char *tz;\n1472|\n1473|  tz = (tz = getenv(\"TZ\")) != NULL ? strdup(tz) : NULL;\n1474|  if (tz != NULL)\n1475|    setenv(\"TZ\", \"\", 1);\n1476|  time_t t = mktime(tm);\n1477|  if (tz != NULL)\n1478|    setenv(\"TZ\", tz, 1);\n1479|  return t;\n1480|#endif\n1481|}\n1482|\n1483|/* Compute and set tm_wday */\n1484|static void set_tm_wday(struct tm *tm) {\n1485|  /*\n1486|   * https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Determination_of_the_day_of_the_week#Gauss.27s_algorithm\n1487|   * https://cs.uwaterloo.ca/~alopez-o/math-faq/node73.html\n1488|   *\n1489|   * Tested with dates from 1900-01-01 through 2100-01-01.  This\n1490|   * algorithm produces the wrong day-of-the-week number for dates in\n1491|   * the range 1900-01-01..1900-02-28, and for 2100-01-01..2100-02-28.\n1492|   * Since this is only needed on OS X and *BSD, we might just document\n1493|   * this.\n1494|   */\n1495|  int century = (1900 + tm->tm_year) / 100;\n1496|  int year = (1900 + tm->tm_year) % 100;\n1497|  if (tm->tm_mon < 2)\n1498|    year--;\n1499|  /*\n1500|   * The month value in the wday computation below is shifted so that\n1501|   * March is 1, April is 2, .., January is 11, and February is 12.\n1502|   */\n1503|  int mon = tm->tm_mon - 1;\n1504|  if (mon < 1)\n1505|    mon += 12;\n1506|  int wday =\n1507|    (tm->tm_mday + (int)floor((2.6 * mon - 0.2)) + year + (int)floor(year / 4.0) + (int)floor(century / 4.0) - 2 * century) % 7;\n1508|  if (wday < 0)\n1509|    wday += 7;\n1510|#if 0\n1511|  /* See commentary above */\n1512|  assert(wday == tm->tm_wday || tm->tm_wday == 8);\n1513|#endif\n1514|  tm->tm_wday = wday;\n1515|}\n1516|/*\n1517| * Compute and set tm_yday.\n1518| *\n1519| */\n1520|static void set_tm_yday(struct tm *tm) {\n1521|  static const int d[] = {0, 31, 59, 90, 120, 151, 181, 212, 243, 273, 304, 334};\n1522|  int mon = tm->tm_mon;\n1523|  int year = 1900 + tm->tm_year;\n1524|  int leap_day = 0;\n1525|  if (tm->tm_mon > 1 &&\n1526|      ((year % 4 == 0 && year % 100 != 0) || (year % 400 == 0)))\n1527|    leap_day = 1;\n1528|\n1529|  /* Bound check index into d[] */\n1530|  if (mon < 0)\n1531|    mon = -mon;\n1532|  if (mon > 11)\n1533|    mon %= 12;\n1534|\n1535|  int yday = d[mon] + leap_day + tm->tm_mday - 1;\n1536|  assert(yday == tm->tm_yday || tm->tm_yday == 367);\n1537|  tm->tm_yday = yday;\n1538|}\n1539|\n1540|static jv f_strptime(jq_state *jq, jv a, jv b) {\n1541|  if (jv_get_kind(a) != JV_KIND_STRING || jv_get_kind(b) != JV_KIND_STRING) {\n1542|    return ret_error2(a, b, jv_string(\"strptime/1 requires string inputs and arguments\"));\n1543|  }\n1544|\n1545|  struct tm tm;\n1546|  memset(&tm, 0, sizeof(tm));\n1547|  tm.tm_wday = 8; // sentinel\n1548|  tm.tm_yday = 367; // sentinel\n1549|  const char *input = jv_string_value(a);\n1550|  const char *fmt = jv_string_value(b);\n1551|\n1552|#ifndef HAVE_STRPTIME\n1553|  if (strcmp(fmt, \"%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ\")) {\n1554|    return ret_error2(a, b, jv_string(\"strptime/1 only supports ISO 8601 on this platform\"));\n1555|  }\n1556|#endif\n1557|  const char *end = strptime(input, fmt, &tm);\n1558|  if (end == NULL || (*end != '\\0' && !isspace((unsigned char)*end))) {\n1559|    return ret_error2(a, b, jv_string_fmt(\"date \\\"%s\\\" does not match format \\\"%s\\\"\", input, fmt));\n1560|  }\n1561|  jv_free(b);\n1562|  /*\n1563|   * This is OS X or some *BSD whose strptime() is just not that\n1564|   * helpful!\n1565|   *\n1566|   * We don't know that the format string did involve parsing a\n1567|   * year, or a month (if tm->tm_mon == 0). The following is based on MurmurHash3.\n1185|     MurmurHash3 was written by Austin Appleby, and is placed\n1186|     in the public domain. */\n1187|\n1188|  const uint8_t* data = (const uint8_t*)str->data;\n1189|  int len = (int)jvp_string_length(str);\n1190|  const int nblocks = len / 4;\n1191|\n1192|  uint32_t h1 = HASH_SEED;\n1193|\n1194|  const uint32_t c1 = 0xcc9e2d51;\n1195| endianness/alignment\n1200|\n1201|    k1 *= c1;\n1202|    k1 = rotl32(k1,15);\n1203|    k1 *= c2;\n1204|\n1205|    h1 ^= k1;\n1206|    h1 = rotl32(h1,13);\n1207|    h1 = h1*5+0xe6546b64;\n1208|  }\n1209|\n1210|  const uint8_t* tail = (const uint8_t*)(data + nblocks*4);\n1211|\n1212|  uint32_t k1 = 0;\n1213|\n1214|  switch(len & 3) {\n1215|  case 3: k1 ^= tail[2] << 16;\n1216| prefix should be added to the output of `halt_error`.\n224|      priv_fwrite(jv_string_value(error_message), jv_string_length_bytes(jv_copy(error_message)),\n225|          stderr, dumpopts & jv_dump_string(error_message, 0);\n230|      fprintf(stderr, \"%s\\n\", jv_string_value(error_message));\n231|    } // else no message on stderr; use --debug-trace to see a message\n232|
